- **Review before committing** (functional checks in headless Chromium plus an adversarial review): all switching paths, the early wakes (press, palette rest, the next tab), the cap on a busy page, the log's Copy/Clear and the ⌘T hand-off timing (median 25ms vs 22ms before) checked; fixed from the review: the Chrome loading-tab wait, held keys, the keep-awake length, repeated palette wakes, the note marked done before it was shown, about:blank first addresses, new windows, the missing reset when the background hands ⌘T off, no way to turn the note back on, and the Copy button (Safari refuses a clipboard write that starts after an await: it now starts in the click with the log as a promise).

## Instant feedback while a switch waits (2026-10-26)

- **Asked (Tirth):** with the wait for the wake (up to 0.4s), nothing showed that a click had registered. Now, as Arc: the tab being switched to is highlighted at once and the page still on screen softly (`behind`, as for ⌘T), and Margin's loading pill shows if the switch is still pending after 150ms (quick switches never flash it).
- **How (`shared/switching.js`, used by `content/core.js` and the new-tab page):** sidebar clicks (tabs, open pins: the tab the background will show, `view.pinTabId`) and palette choices (`content/palette.js reportingSwitches`) start it locally, with their request; next/previous keys get it from the background (`{ type: 'switching', tabId, from }` to the tab on screen, not waited for; Margin's own new-tab page also gets the runtime broadcast, and only the page `from` names acts). The view (`view.sidebar(…, { switchingTo })`) wins over the ⌘T pending highlight, shows a pin open in two tabs by the one it goes to, and treats a switch to a new-tab page as the "+ New Tab" row. It ends when the page is hidden (the switch landed), 300ms after the request is done if the page still shows (the pill goes at once then), and at the latest after 2.5s; a click back on the page's own tab ends it, and the background lets the latest switch win (an earlier one still waiting doesn't land after it). A tab in another window starts nothing here (that window comes forward). The pill shares the page-load pill (`content/loader.js switching`): neither can hide it while the other needs it, and a hidden page drops it without a fade that would play when shown again.
- **Verified:** unit tests (the view, the switching lifecycle with fake timers and document); headless Chromium with Safari's profile: a click moves the highlight in 1–6ms, a busy tab shows the pill at ~150ms and drops it at the switch, palette (tabs, pins, after the ⌘T hand-off), next/previous including held keys (no stale highlight on any page), the start page and its own pill, another window and failed switches (no pill), the page-load pill and the switch pill together (never stuck), a click back during a slow switch lands on the page's own tab; the off-screen WebKit host. Review fixes before committing: the new-tab page's next/previous message, the pill flashing for another window or a failed switch, clicking back, pins open in two tabs, a stale request ending a newer switch, an awaited query on each key press, a switch to a new-tab page.

## The wake's keep-awake could hold a tab awake for minutes (2026-10-27)

- **Found (Tirth asked what held a hidden tab awake for 10.5 minutes in his Safari log):** Margin's own keep-awake script. Of its runs in 8 hours, they ended on time (443 at 0.5s, 251 at 1.0s), but four held their tab's process awake at foreground priority for 138s to 11 minutes, all started between 21:53:37 and 21:54:01, around a reload of Margin, and ending only when their tab closed or reloaded.
- **Cause (reproduced in the WebKit host with Margin's real wake):** WebKit keeps the tab awake until the script's promise settles. In Margin's own world, the script's timer belongs to the page's document; when the page navigates away during the hold (Margin reloads hidden pages after an update), the timer dies and WebKit waits for a garbage collection that may not come for many minutes. Without the navigation the hold ends at 1s.
- **Fix (`background/tabs.js keepAwake`):** it runs in the page's own world (`world: 'MAIN'`; not cleared with Margin's either), and finishes on its timer, on `pagehide`/`pageswap`, or once the tab is shown (awake then anyway; holds on switches are now ~120ms instead of 1s). The page's code shares that world, so the script relies on none of it: a second clock the page can't patch (`AbortSignal.timeout`), every page call guarded, the engine's own promise. Verified in the host: plain 1.0s; ends at navigation (the tab sleeps after the new page loads); ends when shown; github.com with its strict CSP; a page that made `setTimeout` a no-op (1.5s, the second clock) or replaced `Promise` (1.0s). Left open only if a page both cancels `pagehide` and blocks `pageswap` before navigating away.
- **Rejected:** holding with a message to Margin's page script answered after a timer (proposed first): in the host it kept the tab awake through both a navigation and a reload of Margin, worse than the original.

## The narrow rail, off for now (2026-10-27)

- **Asked (Tirth):** disable the narrow icon sidebar (the 52px rail on pages Margin can't fit) for now.
- **How:** one build-profile switch, `platform.compactRail` (false in both builds). With it off, such pages are pushed by the full sidebar again (content/layout.js), the background registers only the full-width first-paint placeholder (`placeholderPlan`), and the start page hides the rail's setting. The rail's code, styles and the saved `compactWhenPushed` setting stay as they were, so setting the switch back to true restores it exactly. The WebKit host's `rail` scenario now checks the full-sidebar push; the unit tests still cover the rail's placeholders with the rail available.

## Arc's sidebar, and folders for pins (2026-10-27)

- **Asked (Tirth):** the sidebar like Arc's (a screenshot of his Arc sidebar), and folders for pinned tabs.
- **Layout (measured from the screenshot, a 2x crop; reconciled from two independent pixel measurements):** the sidebar toggle at the top left (Margin's Keep open / Auto-hide; the fit-this-site switch at the right of that row), the space's name (emoji + name, 13px semibold, muted; double-click or right-click edits the space), one list (the pinned area, then "+ New Tab" and the tabs) that scrolls between the fixed top and a bottom bar (archive at the left: the start page's "Recently archived", or that section on the new-tab page; the spaces' emojis in the middle, the current one in color, the others gray and faded; "+" at the right: New Folder, New Space). Rows 36 high and 5 apart, radius 12, 14px text in Arc's warm near-black; favicon 16, 10 before the title; folders: Arc's two-tone folder icon and the name in semibold, their pins indented 16; the tab shown an opaque #EBEBEB pill with a soft shadow, hover a faint dark tint, × on hover. Compared side by side at the same scale: rows within 1px, icons the same boxes. This replaces SPEC's order (space switcher at the top) for the Arc layout Tirth asked for. Unchanged: the sidebar's gray, the page frame, the width setting (Arc's is 228; Margin's default stays 260, drag to 230), the first-paint placeholders (they only depend on width and frame), the right-click menus' look.
- **Pinned tabs, as Arc:** an open pinned tab's × closes the tab and keeps the pin (Unpin moved to its menu); closed pins are no longer dimmed.
- **Folders (state):** a new stored list, `folders: [{ id, spaceId, name, open, pos }]` (SPEC's state shape gains it); a pin may have `folderId` and `pos`. Each space's top level orders folders and loose pins together by `pos`, each folder its own pins; pins saved before folders keep their order (their place in the list stands in for `pos`, in the model and in pages, which read raw storage). Changes renumber only what they touch and save nothing when nothing changes (every save redraws every page's sidebar). Deleting a folder keeps its pins, in its place; deleting a space moves its folders and pins to the first space (a pin whose URL that space already has goes, as before). New folders get distinct names ("New Folder 2") since menus name them.
- **Folders (UI):** click a folder (or Enter/Space on it) to open or close it; a closed one still shows the pin of the tab being shown, so the highlight never disappears; double-click renames it in place (Enter saves, Escape cancels, clicking away saves; the field keeps its text and caret while other pages change the state); right-click: Rename, Open/Close, Delete (pins stay). Pins: Unpin, Move to 📁 folder, Remove from Folder, New Folder with Pin, Close Tab. Tabs: Pin Tab, Pin to 📁 folder, New Folder with Tab, Move to space…, Close Tab. Drag and drop: a tab or pin onto a folder goes into it (a tab gets pinned there), between rows before or after that row (a gap counts as the row below; folders stay at the top level), a pin onto the tabs unpins (its tab stays open), a tab onto a space's emoji moves it there; an empty pinned area shows "Drop to pin" while a tab is dragged. Next/previous tab follow the rows shown (a closed folder's tabs are skipped). The palette shows a pin's folder with its space.
- **Verified:** unit tests (folders: order, moves, rename, open/close, delete, deleting a space, old data, distinct names, nothing-changed saves; the view: grouping, closed folders, next/previous); headless Chromium: every interaction above through the real UI (55 checks), drag edge cases (a drop just above itself stays put, gaps, a redraw mid-drag), narrow (160) and wide (500) sidebars, 50+ pins scrolling with the saved position, the hover sidebar staying open while renaming or dragging (and hiding after, which now also works for the space editor), the fitted layout, the new-tab page; side-by-side render against the screenshot; the off-screen WebKit host. Review fixes before committing: the drop math (before itself, gaps), a stuck drag after a redraw, double-click also toggling the folder, the rail's rules (it's off) losing to the new ones, the bottom bar's current space out of view with many spaces, long menus, keyboard order through closed folders, repeated view building, unscoped styles, accessibility of folder rows.

## Switching as instant as Arc's; a space shows its last tab (2026-10-27)

- **Asked (Tirth, with recordings of Arc and Margin):** switching between two tabs wasn't as instant as in Arc. Measured frame by frame (AVFoundation; ffmpeg isn't installed): Arc swaps the highlight in one frame at the click, no in-between state, no tooltip. Margin showed a native title tooltip on rows, moved the highlight in two steps (the new row at once, the old one only to the soft "behind" state), the page arrived up to ~150ms later (varying; some switches in the same frame), and the first frame of the new page drew a stale hover and × on the wrong row. Safari's log for the same moment: from Margin's wake scripts to the page visible <20ms ("Selecting tab" 1ms after the scripts), so the delay was Margin's: the message to the background, then tabs.get and permissions.contains before the wake. Tirth clicks by tap (the wake started only ~15ms before each switch), so switching on mouse-down wouldn't gain much, and it would break dragging rows (the sidebar lives in the page, unlike Chrome's tab strip).
- **Changes:** while switching, the highlight moves whole (the tab still on screen plain; the new page's sidebar then looks the same, so the sidebar switch is instant whenever the page lands); "behind" stays only for the ⌘T palette. No native tooltips on rows (they keep aria labels; the × too). A page shown again draws no hover until the pointer moves there (`hover-stale`). Resting on a row 80ms wakes its tab and renews a fresh wake every 500ms (stopping on leave, click, drag, the page hidden, or 10s without movement; found again by id after redraws), so a click switches with nothing to wait for: headless, the activateTab message to tabs.update in 0–7ms with no lookups. The wake reads the tab from Margin's loaded state (the browser only to confirm "already shown"), and remembers per site that Margin has access (not a "no": a grant from Safari's own controls may come without an event).
- **Spaces (Arc):** showing a space (its emoji in the bottom bar, the palette, ⌃⌘←/→) brings its most recent tab in that window to the front; with none there, the untouched start or new-tab page in front moves to it, else Margin's start page opens in it. Held space keys change only the sidebar until the last press. A tab of another space brought to the front (the palette, Safari's tab bar) switches the window to that space. Closing the tab in front stays in its space: the sidebar's × brings the space's most recent other tab first (no flash); with ⌘W, when the browser picks a tab of another space, Margin goes back to the closed tab's space and its most recent tab (whichever of the two events Safari sends first). Pins open only tabs of their own space (one elsewhere would switch spaces). A new space is shown (its start page) once its name editor closes. A tab Margin opens cancels a switch still waiting for its wake (two quick space switches ended on the wrong space). Double-click no longer edits a space that isn't the current one (its first click shows it); right-click does.
- **Verified:** unit tests (showSpace: most recent tab, same window, kept when already in the space, a closed candidate, the start page opened or moved, sidebar only; the wake's state use, a stale "shown", the access cache; pins; following a tab's space); headless Chromium: the highlight moving whole in the first mutation after a click, ⌘T still "behind", no row tooltips, hover-stale's CSS, hover wakes and renewals through redraws, clicks finding a wake, 30 space-switching checks, closing via the sidebar and ⌘W, the quick-switch race; the off-screen WebKit host.

## A tab still running the previous copy after an update (2026-10-27)

- **Reported (Tirth, a recording):** from one tab, every click on another tab in the sidebar highlighted it (the old tab softly: that tab ran an older build), showed the pill, never switched, and ~2.5s later the highlight went back. Safari's log for those 17 seconds: nothing reached Margin's background (no wake scripts, no "Selecting tab") after the switch that brought that tab to the front; its web process predated the reinstall at 01:07 (a dev rebuild), and its sidebar was an older build's. So: the tab was asleep during the update, never got the new copy (injectIntoOpenTabs skips sleeping tabs), and still ran the previous one, whose messages in WebKit neither arrive nor fail (confirmed in the WebKit host: a pre-update copy's runtime.sendMessage stays unanswered, and the new install's content-script world is fresh).
- **Fix:** whenever a tab comes to the front (or its window gains focus), the background checks once per run that the current copy runs there (Margin.content in Margin's own content-script world; an earlier install's lives in another world) and, if not, injects the content scripts: the new copy takes the page over from the stale sidebar (existing takeover). Headless: the stale tab's new sidebar ~0.45s after it came to the front, its clicks switching in ~25ms; the WebKit host shows the same recovery. Safety net in the page: a message gets no answer in time (switches 1.5s, others 8s): it's given up (the switch stops waiting: ~1.8s instead of hanging 2.5s), and only after 8s of silence, on the page in front with Margin on it, a note offers to reload (a GET of the URL, no form resent), taken back if the answer comes after all. A fitted page taken over this way (its layout stays the old copy's) gets the reload offer at once.
- **Not verified in Safari:** whether a suspended tab skips WebKit's own injection of the new copy at an update (the host can't suspend a process); Safari's log was consistent with it. Development rebuilds make this common; App Store updates make it rarer.

## An empty screen while switching tabs (2026-10-27)

- **Asked (Tirth, with a screenshot of Arc's empty tab):** the moment a tab is clicked, the page being left shouldn't stay on screen while the other one comes up: cover it with an empty screen and a loader in the middle, like Arc.
- **How:** the switch Margin already tracks (shared/switching.js: a click in the sidebar or palette, next/previous) now also turns on a cover in Arcadia (chrome.js `createSwitchCover`): the sidebar's gray lightened a little, over the page card while docked or fitted (same inset and corners as the card) and over the whole page under a hovered sidebar; on Margin's new-tab page over its card. It comes on in the same frame as the highlight, with no fade; the spinner (28px ring, track and arc) shows only after 160ms, so quick switches are a plain blank. It goes when the page is hidden (the switch landed), with no fade, so the page never shows it again when you come back; if the switch fails, it fades out (90ms) when the switch state ends (300ms after the answer, 2.5s at most). While on, it takes clicks meant for the page left behind. It replaces the loading pill for switches in Arcadia (the pill remains for page loads, and for switches in a theme without a cover). Not for ⌘T or the new-tab palette (no switch).
- **Next/previous keys:** the cover comes only once the switch takes longer than 150ms, and not at all while more presses wait: a held key passes through each tab in ~130ms, and covering them at once would turn the run into gray cards (found in review). The background now tells the page when a key switch is done or failed (`switch-done`, `switch-failed`; shared/switching.js `backgroundSwitches`), which it treats like the answer to its own click: failed ends it at once (the tab closed meanwhile: before, 2.5s of cover), done ends it 300ms later if the page still shows (Chrome's split view keeps the page left on screen; before, the cover stayed 2.5s).
- **Accepted:** the cover is the last picture of the page left (on purpose: it is what shows while the next tab draws), so Safari's tab overview or previews may show it gray, and coming back may show it for a frame before the page repaints (the cover is removed while the page is hidden). Removing it before the switch would show the old page again, which is what this replaces.
- **Verified:** unit tests (pass-through and failure messages); headless Chromium with a held switch (1.5s): docked, fitted and auto-hide layouts and the new-tab page: the cover on in the click's own task, the spinner at ~440ms, gone after; screenshots compared. A held key across four tabs: no page covered. One slow key press (600ms): the cover at ~165ms, off 300ms after the switch where the page stays visible. A failed key switch: cover off ~0.27s after. The WebKit host's suite. Three review rounds, each finding verified. **Not verified in Safari:** the return frame above.

## Swipe between spaces (2026-10-27)

- **Asked (Tirth):** switch spaces by swiping left and right with two fingers in the sidebar, as in Arc. (Part of a wider wish: spaces in different Safari profiles in one window. Not possible: a Safari window belongs to one profile and no extension API, in Safari, Chrome or Edge, creates, opens or switches profiles; researched with WebKit/Chromium source and off-screen tests. Separate logins per space within one profile, by swapping a site's login cookies and with Google account hints, is the possible route, to be tried next.)
- **How:** sideways wheel events over the sidebar (a trackpad: pixel deltas) drag the space's part of the sidebar (its name and list, now one `.space-pane`) with the fingers, and the neighbouring space slides in beside it: a still copy of it, drawn when the swipe starts, with its most recent tab highlighted as it will be. Letting go past 30% of the width, or with a flick (≥ 40px at ≥ 0.6px/ms), shows that space (220ms slide), else it slides back. No wrapping: past the first or last space the list resists (a rubber band); ⌃⌘←/→ still wrap. On release the sidebar shows the new space at once (a local override until the state says so, 3s at most) and asks the background to show it, which brings its most recent tab to the front, with the switch highlight and cover (or opens the start page in an empty space), as clicking its icon does. Swiped (or its icon clicked) on the sidebar out over the page with Auto-hide, the sidebar stays out on the page that comes to the front, as for a tab clicked there (`setSpace` with `show`, and the list position saved first). Web pages get no trackpad phases, so the fingers lifting is inferred as for pull-to-refresh: momentum's smooth decay, or a 140ms gap (shared/space-swipe.js). Vertical scrolling, mouse wheels (line deltas or big steps), pinches, the spaces bar when it scrolls sideways, and the sidebar while editing, renaming, dragging, resizing or with a menu open are left alone. The swipe's events are cancelled, so neither Safari's back/forward swipe nor Margin's own fires from the sidebar. Works on web pages (while the sidebar is out) and on the new-tab page.
- **Review fixes before committing (three rounds):** the momentum after a flick keeps counting as that gesture while the space slides into place (it started a second swipe and could skip a space); a drag that stops without lifting is let go when the next gesture starts (it could stay half-way); drawing the neighbours' pictures no longer moves the list's or the spaces bar's scroll; redraws wait until the slide ends (one would redraw the leaving space as the new one); the new space's list starts at its top, also for the window's other pages; swiping back to this page's own space stops a switch still pending; the shown space falls back to the state's after 3s on its own; fingers down again during the momentum (a delta growing, turning back, or a vertical scroll) start a new gesture at once instead of being held; a swipe started while the last one slides in is shown from where it's first drawn; the list's top is saved before the switch, so the next page opens the new space at its top; the neighbours' pictures keep the letter for an icon that doesn't load; an oversized first step (skipped) no longer shifts the drag's start; mouse wheels don't swipe (Shift+wheel, wheels whose notches WebKit/Chromium report as 120s, and anything under 4 events). Accepted: a Mac mouse's sideways wheel (40px notches look like a trackpad's) spun fast can switch.
- **Verified:** unit tests (axis lock, following, release on decay and on a gap, momentum ignored however long, a drag let go by the next gesture, mouse wheels and pinches, settle and rubber band); headless Chromium with trackpad-like wheel events (Playwright's own are mouse notches, rejected: checked): a swipe to the next space shows it with its tab in front, a short slow one stays, the previous space, the rubber band at the first, vertical scroll, a realistic 10ms flick with momentum, the new-tab page; screenshots mid-swipe and at the edge; the WebKit host's new `spaces` scenario (short swipe stays, flick switches and brings its tab forward). Not verified in Safari: the feel with a real trackpad.

## Margin's UI behaves as the browser's own (2026-10-27)

- **Asked (Tirth):** zoom only in the page (not the sidebar or anywhere else), no selecting, highlighting or copying the sidebar's text, and no right-click menu on the sidebar except Margin's own on the tab list: "make it feel like an actual browser".
- **Zoom (checked first in the WebKit host, now with page zoom: `{ op: 'zoom' }`):** Safari's page zoom (⌘+ / ⌘−, and Margin's pinch, which sets it) scaled Margin's UI with the page: at 150% the sidebar was 1.5× as wide. Now Margin's hosts are scaled back by 1/zoom (CSS `zoom`; shared/dom.js `setPageZoom`), so the sidebar, frame, palette, cover and badges keep their size, and the page is pushed, or its fitted card placed, by the sidebar's width in the page's zoomed pixels (content/layout.js; also the reveal zone and the hover edge). The zoom is read from the browser (`tabs.getZoom`) at start, after a resize (a zoom change resizes the page's viewport), when the page is shown again, and after Margin's pinch. Measured in WebKit under CSS zoom: boxes and pointer positions stay in the page's pixels, offset/client sizes are the element's, viewport units are scaled by the zoom (so Margin's are written `calc(100vh * var(--page-zoom, 1))`), and hit-testing matches what's drawn. The sidebar converts pointer positions where they set its own sizes (resizing, menus, the swipe's pictures, the spaces bar). A pinch that starts over Margin's UI zooms nothing. Margin's new-tab page: the launcher zooms, its sidebar and frame don't.
- **Selection, dragging, Look Up:** Margin's UI (sidebar and frame, palette, new-tab page) isn't selectable, its icons and links can't be dragged out (its own rows, pins and folders still drag), and a force click doesn't open Look Up; text fields work as before.
- **Context menus:** no browser menu anywhere on Margin's UI, its frame and its new-tab page; Margin's own menus (tabs, pins, folders; the space name and icons' editor) stay, and text fields keep the browser's (cut/copy/paste). On web pages the right-click doesn't reach the page either (some sites open their own menu for any right-click). The frame's strips around the page card now take the pointer, as a browser's frame does (invisible strips; the card's rounded corners stay the page's).
- **Verified:** unit tests (a pinch over Margin is flagged); the WebKit host's new `zoom` scenario (150%: Margin scaled back, pushed by 260/1.5, back to normal at 100%, the fitted card at 260/1.5, the new-tab sidebar), screenshots at 100% and 150%; headless Chromium (the same zoom checks; right-clicks on the name, bar and a tab row, selection and ⌃+wheel blocked, the tab row's own menu opens, the page's listeners don't see the right-click, the strips take the pointer and the card doesn't; the new-tab page's field keeps its menu and selection).
- **Review fixes before committing (two rounds):** each site's zoom is remembered (storage `pageZoom`, origin → [zoom, when]; only sites zoomed away from the browser's default zoom, which is kept once; the 150 most recently changed; written by the background only when it changes, since every write redraws Margin's pages), so a zoomed site's pages draw Margin at its size from their first render instead of snapping when the browser answers; the sidebar's swipe and spaces-bar wheel use the sidebar's own px (Chromium's wheel deltas are in the page's zoomed px, WebKit's in screen points: `platform.zoomedWheel`; WebCore's WheelEvent passes the platform's deltas on unscaled); the palette's short-window layouts use the window's height in Margin's px (also when the window resizes while it's open); the mouse-wheel test uses the unzoomed delta; Safari's own pinch is cancelled over Margin even when Margin's gesture handling is off; the palette takes the current zoom each time it opens; ⌃+wheel still zooms the new-tab page's launcher; a press on the frame closes Margin's menu, and the strips stop taking the pointer as the frame fades out; the hosts themselves aren't selectable (`all: initial` reset the `:host` rule); only real text fields (not checkboxes, selects or read-only fields) keep the browser's menu.
- **Limits:** before the first visit at a zoom, and for the first-paint placeholder (it can't know the zoom), a zoomed site's page is pushed by the unzoomed width for a moment; Safari's two-finger double-tap "smart zoom" can't be blocked by a page (System Settings › Trackpad turns it off); a page's own capture-phase listeners still see right-clicks on Margin.

## Tab ids change: tabs keep their spaces (2026-10-27)

- **Asked (Tirth):** do Safari's tab ids change over time? Checked in the WebKit host: a tab keeps its id while it sleeps and wakes, reloads or navigates; every tab gets a new id when Margin is updated (a new copy: 42 → 90; every Xcode rebuild, later App Store updates), and after a browser restart (already known: ids reused with other meanings).
- **Bug found:** Margin keeps each tab's space by id. After an update or a restart it rebuilt spaces only from pinned tabs, so every other tab fell into one space (the host reproduced it: a tab in Work was in Personal after an update; with the fix it stays in Work, and it fails again without it).
- **Fix (`state/model.js` `matchTabs`, `syncTabs`):** open tabs Margin doesn't know (all of them after a restart) are matched to the records of tabs that are gone, by address: window by window (each open window paired with the old one it shares most addresses with), duplicates in tab order, then by address in any window. A matched tab keeps its space and record; after a restart its last use is moved forward so the most recently used is now and the gaps stay (the space's most recent tab is still right; nothing is archived just for the time the browser was closed). A window Margin doesn't know shows the space of the tab it shows. Unmatched tabs go to a pin's space with that address (after a restart) or their window's space. Known ids are never re-matched, and matching happens only when the ids were renumbered (a restart, or no open tab is one Margin knows): on a plain wake a new tab is new. New-tab pages, blank and empty addresses only match in order within windows paired by their web pages (an empty new window doesn't open in an old space).
- **Later restores (five review rounds; redesigned in the second):** Safari opens with a new window by default; reopening the last session (History › Reopen All Windows from Last Session, ⌘⇧T) brings the old tabs after Margin's sync. Old tabs no open tab matched are kept a day by their old window (`lostTabs`, with how many web pages that window had; at most 300; merged, never wiped). Only tabs that may be coming back are offered (background/restore.js), for a minute after their window was first watched: after a sync that renumbered the tabs, every window's tabs not matched yet and the ones created in it later; a window new to Margin while old tabs wait; tabs created in a watched window only in its first 10 seconds (a window is restored in one burst), never new-tab pages (a tab coming back has its address), never tabs the user had before or dragged into a window of their own, and never a tab put in a space on purpose (by the user, or by Margin opening it there: `placed`). Old windows are told apart per renumbering (window ids repeat). Once they stop arriving or changing (1.5s) they're matched to the old windows as at a restart (`reclaimTabs`): window to old window only, needing two of a multi-tab old window's pages (counting those already back in that window; one for a one-tab window), so a page opened anew (a link in a new window, ⌘N then typing) doesn't take an old tab's place. The tab in front keeps its last use, the others get theirs moved forward by the time since (nothing archived for the time in between); the window shows its front tab's space. A worker woken by a reopened window takes them back in its own sync. Duplicates of a page pair with the nearest by position. Deleting a space moves its waiting tabs to the space its tabs go to. Rejected along the way: claiming tab by tab with flags (claims by any link for a day, a start page claiming when typed in, stale last use archiving reopened tabs, one unmatched tab blocking its window, the pool wiped), and re-watching windows at every worker wake (it took the user's own tabs back into old spaces). Accepted: within a minute of a reopen or restart, a new window taken to the only page of a one-tab window from before goes to that window's space; what's still waiting to be taken back is kept in the background's memory, so a background unloaded within that minute loses it (the tabs stay in their window's space).
- **Verified:** unit tests (a restart with reused ids, duplicates, two windows, the order of use; an update without a fresh session; an unmatched tab joining its window's space or a pin's; a plain wake not matching; a session reopened hours after the sync; a link in a new window not taking an old tab's place; a tab whose address comes later; a worker woken by the reopened window; new-tab pages not matching across windows; the day's expiry; a deleted space; the background watcher end to end with the fake browser); the WebKit host's new `tabids` scenario (same ids through sleep, wake and reload; after an update new ids and the same spaces). Not verified in Safari: a real restart (the host can't restart Safari).

## ⌘T with no flicker: the browser's New Tab moved off ⌘T (2026-10-27)

- **Asked (Tirth):** can Margin take ⌘T from Safari so the screen doesn't flicker? Safari keeps ⌘T for File › New Tab and never gives it to extensions or pages: the tab exists before Margin hears of it (the hand-off closes it again: the flicker). The way out is on the Mac's side: System Settings › Keyboard › Keyboard Shortcuts › App Shortcuts › Safari, "New Tab" → another shortcut (Tirth set it). ⌘T then reaches the page like any key.
- **Margin's side (`content/palette.js installNewTabKey`):** a plain ⌘T on a page (or in the fitted layout's card) opens the palette to choose a new tab at once, "+ New Tab" highlighted, with no tab created: Escape leaves nothing behind, choosing opens the one new tab. On Margin's new-tab page it does what "+ New Tab" does there. In Chrome outside full screen (Margin out of the way) it opens a new tab as usual. Without the remap nothing changes (the browser never lets ⌘T reach the page; the hand-off stays).
- **App-wide (Safari, Tirth's choice):** where Margin's script doesn't run (Safari's own pages, PDFs, sites without permission) or the address bar has focus, the page never sees ⌘T. An extension shortcut works app-wide, but WebKit allows four and Margin used all four (the ⌃⌘ arrows); a fifth keeps its command, loses its key and records a manifest error (WebKit `WebExtension::populateCommandsIfNeeded`). So Safari's manifest gives ⌘T (`new-tab`, `Command+T`) the slot of ⌃⌘← (previous space), which still works on web pages (Margin's page listener) but no longer in Safari's own pages or the address bar. The background (`handlers.js newTabKey`): the page in front opens its palette (the same `show-palette` as the hand-off, 1s at most); Margin's new-tab page does its "+ New Tab"; anything else gets a new tab with Margin's new-tab page (its palette opens there, as Safari's ⌘T did). Chrome keeps the four arrows (it refuses some shortcuts outright, and then the whole extension). Checked in the WebKit host: the manifest loads with no errors, `new-tab` is t + Command, the arrows ⌃⌘, prev-space none. Whether Safari delivers it with File › New Tab remapped is Tirth's check (TESTING.md).
- **Verified:** unit test (only plain ⌘T, once per press, never the page's); headless Chromium (pushed and fitted layouts, the new-tab page): the palette opens, the tab count stays 1, Escape leaves no tab, choosing opens one.

## ⌥K dropped: ⌘T is the palette's key (2026-10-27)

- **Asked (Tirth):** with ⌘T working without a flicker, drop ⌥K: it opens the same palette.
- **Changed:** the ⌥K listener (pages, the fitted card, the new-tab page) and its setting (`paletteShortcut`, dropped from saved settings like other removed ones; its Settings field) are gone. ⌘T is the palette's key: on pages and the new-tab page once the browser's New Tab has another shortcut, app-wide in Safari through its `new-tab` command, and otherwise through the hand-off of the browser's new tab (Chrome without the remap: the palette still opens, with the old flicker). The keyless `open-palette` command stays (Chrome lets users give it a key in its shortcuts page). Settings refuse ⌘T for another shortcut ("already used for New tab").
- **Verified:** unit tests (the default and a saved ⌥K setting gone); headless Chromium (⌥K opens nothing, ⌘T the palette, Settings without the field, ⌘T refused for the sidebar's shortcut); the WebKit host's suite. E2E tests updated to ⌘T, not run.

## Zoom: followed at once, and read reliably (2026-10-27)

- **Found:** the WebKit suite failed intermittently in its zoom scenario (and once in switching, under load: 7 reruns passed). Measured in the host: after ⌘+, Margin applied the zoom 87–1296ms later, the time of a round trip to a background still busy after starting; until then the sidebar showed at the page's zoom. Two bugs on top: the page's zoom read was the one message sent without `send`'s retry (a background asleep when asked never answered it), and the new-tab page asked through the background, where an extension page may not count as a tab (the host answered "no zoom").
- **Fixed:** pages follow a zoom change in the resize itself: `devicePixelRatio` is the screen's scale times the page's zoom (WebKit and Chromium; 2 → 3 at 150% in the host), and each answer from the browser gives the screen's scale, so the next change needs no round trip (the answer, a moment later, confirms it; moving to another display is corrected by it). Measured: 14–23ms after the change, in the same frame as the resize. The read keeps `send`'s retry; the new-tab page asks the browser directly (`tabs.getZoom`).
- **Verified:** the zoom scenario 8 times in a row, switching 5 times, the whole suite; unit tests. (The commit before, dropping ⌥K, was pushed before the suite's result was read; that run's failures were these.)

## A set-up window in Margin's Mac app (2026-10-28)

- **Asked (Tirth):** Margin needs a lot of configuring in Safari; an app to onboard and configure people, Safari first, Chrome and Edge later.
- **Built:** the converter's containing app is now Margin's set-up window (`app/safari/ViewController.swift`, SwiftUI hosted in the storyboard's window; `scripts/make-xcode.sh` copies it and the handler in, sets their IDs, and raises the deployment target to macOS 14). Required steps, each checked: Margin on (`SFSafariExtensionManager`, polled), allowed on every website, new windows and tabs open Margin's page, ⌘T reaches Margin (Safari's New Tab moved to another App Shortcut). Recommended steps (reopen last session's windows, tidy Safari's own UI) are ticked by hand; a shortcut sheet, with a Karabiner rule turning ⌥⌘ arrows (Safari's) into ⌃⌘ arrows. Buttons open Safari's extension settings, Safari, and Keyboard settings; tests ("press ⌘T", "⌘N, then +") wait for what the extension sees and say what's wrong.
- **How the app learns it:** only the extension sees most of it. `background/onboarding.js` (Safari only, `platform.nativeApp`; `nativeMessaging` added to Safari's manifest) sends `browser.runtime.sendNativeMessage` messages: whether `<all_urls>` is granted, and event names (`cmdTKey`, `safariNewTab`, `marginNewWindowPage`, `marginNewTabPage`, `otherNewTabPage`, `homepageNewTab`). The appex (`SafariWebExtensionHandler.swift`) posts them as a distributed notification while the app is running (both are sandboxed with no shared storage; a string object is what a sandboxed process may post) and replies whether the app is open. Distributed notifications reach every process in the session: so only names and the access flag go, no addresses or page content, nothing while the app is closed, and other processes could fake a step (they gain nothing). App groups would avoid that but need a signed team build.
- **What counts (after review):** the app can't call the extension, so events always go (user actions: ⌘T, a new tab or window; one per name a second); access goes when it changes (at once) and again until the app has heard it (page loads, backing off 15s → 5min while the app is closed, at once when an event finds it open). Tabs Margin opens itself (⌘T where the page has no palette, new windows) report nothing (`tabs.js openedByMargin`). ⌘T is done once it reached Margin, and undone only by a failed ⌘T test: Safari's own New Tab also runs from its + button and File menu, so `safariNewTab` alone says nothing. New windows and new tabs are checked separately (a window's first tab vs another), and count only since Safari last launched and Margin was last seen off: the unsigned build is turned off at each launch and Safari's new-tab setting falls back (the "⌘T opening Safari's homepage" entry). `otherNewTabPage` (one hit: could be a link from another app) only fails a running test; `homepageNewTab` (the same page twice, newtab-hint's heuristic) marks new tabs to do. The turn-on step says how to allow unsigned extensions.
- **Limits:** Safari's Start Page, Favorites or an empty page as "New tabs open with" sends nothing, so a test times out naming the missing setting rather than failing at once (when the other half came, the missing one is marked to do). With macOS's "Prefer tabs: Always", ⌘N opens a tab, so the new-window half can't be confirmed (a window counts by its first tab). ⌃⌘← (previous space) works on web pages only (its key went to ⌘T); the sheet says so.
- **Verified:** an unsigned `xcodebuild` of the regenerated project (no warnings); off-screen renders of the window (fresh, part-way, done in dark); a logic harness over the model (25 checks: step states, the tests, staleness, persistence); unit tests for the reporting. Not run in the real Safari (needs the app run from Xcode): TESTING.md.

## Switches that land or say why, one copy of Margin in charge, and getting ready after a launch (2026-10-28)

- **Asked (Tirth):** after opening Safari, a click on another tab highlighted it and showed the loader, but nothing came and after 2–3 s the highlight went back; tabs sometimes looked as if they were in another space; "the reload doesn't work". He asked for a full-window loader while Margin gets ready, switches that wait under the loader until the next tab is loaded and its sidebar is the same as the one left, proper tab tracking, and nothing going back silently.
- **Found** (his Safari's log, read passively; WebKit's source; the WebKit host):
  - The four switches right after his 13:57 launch each ran their scripts into a tab Safari hadn't loaded yet, and no tab came to the front after any of them. A page's switch gave up after 1.5 s and its highlight went back without a word.
  - Right after a launch or an update, pages draw from storage whose tab ids are the previous copy's until the background has matched the open tabs; a click then names a tab this copy doesn't have.
  - Copies of Margin don't hear each other's storage writes: WebKit sends `storage.onChanged` only within the copy that wrote. Tab and window ids are each copy's own. In the host, an old copy (main's build) running beside the installed one left `tabs` holding ids the installed one doesn't have. Two copies loaded at once share one script world in pages: a page runs one of them.
  - Writing several storage keys isn't one write (one row per key, stopping at the first error); his log had SQLite error 5 (busy) on two writes at 13:49.
  - Margin's storage file (LocalStorage.db) was missing at every Safari launch that day, in all three of his profiles: likely Safari clearing the unsigned build's data at relaunch. Not confirmed with him yet.
  - The cleanup reload after an update (e7f30af) asked again every half second, forever, for hidden pages with a `#` in their address and for the tab in front of a minimized window.
  - A switch to a tab Safari had unloaded: its page drew Margin before its background said which tab it was, so it showed the previous tab highlighted, in that tab's space.
  - My scratch build of the Mac app from the set-up work was still registered with macOS beside the one Xcode runs (Safari logged "Other version in use"); unregistered and deleted.
- **Changed:**
  - **One copy in charge** (`shared/owner.js`, `background/store.js`): storage key `owner` = { origin, since }, `since` being the copy's first background start (kept in its own `storage.session`). The newest copy owns the state; any other writes nothing and its listeners do nothing, and it finds out at its next write or when a page starts (no event reaches it). Its pages stand back, saying "Quit and reopen Safari to finish updating it".
  - **The open tabs in one value** (`shared/stored.js`): `openTabs` = { origin, synced, tabs, tabSpace, activeSpaceByWindow }, so pages never see tabs without their spaces, and know whose ids they are and whether they're matched yet. The old names are read once and never written (older copies still write them); `lostTabs` is `lostTabs2`.
  - **Getting ready** (`content/ready.js`): while the stored tabs aren't this copy's and matched, a page shown meanwhile is covered, sidebar included ("Getting your tabs ready…"), after 250 ms, for at least 400 ms, at most 6 s; not over a page in use when Margin updates. Requests that name a tab wait for the background's first sync (at most 5 s), except on a background restarting within the same copy.
  - **Switches** (`background/tabs.js`, `handlers.js`, `shared/switching.js`): a switch request carries the row's address, place and title, so a tab id from before still finds its tab; and when the page stops waiting, so the background never switches after that. Preparing the next tab's sidebar may take up to 900 ms (a suspended process takes a median 279 ms to come back in his log). A page still running an earlier copy gets the current one first. After switching, Margin checks the tab is in front, and asks once more. A switch that didn't happen ends at once and says why, instead of going back.
  - **Tabs Safari unloaded:** noted in storage before they're shown (`showing`), so the page draws its own tab highlighted, its space, and the sidebar open and scrolled as before, from its first frame; the fitted layout stops holding it back; and the switch's cover stays over the page, its spinner already turning, until it has loaded (at most 2.5 s), so the switch reads as one wait. Loading it before it's shown isn't possible (below).
  - **Pages:** hidden ones keep their state current from the change events and draw at once when shown; a page shows its own tab's space until the state records it's in front; key switches save the sidebar's scroll first.
  - **Reloads:** the update reload answers whether to try again; `#` pages get Safari's reload; the front tab of a minimized window may be reloaded; at most six tries, once per 30 s per tab. The notice's Reload button works on `#` pages; pull-to-refresh stays on after a takeover.
- **After review** (five reviewers, each finding checked by a second one; 48 of 53 held up):
  - A storage error while deciding who owns the state no longer sticks (tried again); a copy that loses the state between making a change and writing it fails the change (auto-archive then closes nothing it couldn't archive, and a page is told); every listener checks ownership again first, at most once a second; a copy claimed over by an older one keeps its tabs matched; a clock that was ahead can't lock every copy out; the first sync is tried again if it fails.
  - From this build on every copy marks its sidebar, so a copy taking over finds marked sidebars too: once it owns the state it hides them and arms the cleanup reload, as before for unmarked ones (otherwise the next update would never clean up), and shows its own again if another hid them.
  - Requests that name a tab (switching, closing, moving, pinning) say whose ids the page drew: an id from another copy or another session is never acted on, only the tab found at its address. Switch requests wait 7 s and the background never switches after that; a tab chosen in Safari's own tab bar meanwhile wins; a switch that gets no answer says so.
  - The note of a tab being shown goes once its page has said hello, isn't taken by a reload, and is undone if the page turns out to be another tab. The rule that a page shows its own tab's space applies only for 2 s after it's shown: a sidebar showing a space of its own choosing (a swipe) keeps it (the `spaces` scenario failed 1 in 6 without this; 0 in 10 with).
  - The update reload: asks the page whether it's hidden before reloading the tab in front of an unfocused window; retries a failed or unanswered request; Reload works on addresses ending in a bare `#`.
  - Chrome: `openTabs` records its writer's start too, so pages trust last session's tab ids only until hello says otherwise. The Chrome E2E tests read the new storage names (not run).
  - Not changed: copies from before this build still write spaces, pins and settings under the shared names until Safari quits (quit Safari once after installing); a small window between a copy's check and its write remains.
- **After a second review** (of 3cf59fd; 18 findings, all held up, mostly low):
  - A storage error while deciding who owns the state answers an error, not `stale` (that would have hidden every asking page's sidebar for good), and the first sync tries again then too.
  - The ownership check as an event arrives isn't waited for (events could run out of order, bringing back a closed tab or an old title); every write still checks, and checks run one at a time.
  - A record from a clock that was ahead is claimed only by a copy starting, so two running copies can't claim it from each other in turn; a copy claimed back from an older one writes its tabs again at once, still marked matched.
  - Margin's own activations don't count as the user's when their event comes late (that cancelled the next switch). A page's tab id is trusted only from tabs marked matched. A space switch that fails puts the window back in the space it showed.
  - Pages: a takeover of a marked sidebar no longer turns off this copy's swipes; a reload notice that went by itself no longer silences later failed switches; a retried switch keeps its deadline; the startup cover runs its full time once per wait, not at every show; a wrongly adopted note redraws from storage; the note is taken by a page that started as it was written, whatever kind of load (not by the navigation type); a request naming the page's own tab has no hint.
  - The new-tab page: the palette and pin tiles send hints and deadlines like the sidebar, and it shows its own tab's space for 2 s after it's shown, until a switch is asked for from it (on web pages too: a flick right after a page was shown went back to the page's own space as it landed; the `spaces` scenario caught it).
  - Checked once more (4 of 5 held up): listeners wait for the ownership check again (a replaced copy acted on the event that told it, a ⌘T opening a second tab), with checks one at a time so events still go on in order; the clock rule only on a copy's first start, not each time its background wakes; the startup cover's limit counts for the whole wait, and pages don't wait on a copy that started more than 20 s ago.
- **Rejected:** loading a tab Safari unloaded in the background (Safari ignores `tabs.reload` for it, and `tabs.update` with its address would lose its history and scroll); a per-tab key in the page's sessionStorage (Safari's restore of it timed out at every launch in his log); rewriting the tab matching (a property test, 2000 random sessions renumbered in order, put every tab back in its space); writing the state back when another copy overwrote it (no event reaches it); the other assistant's storage envelope (4356284) and quiet takeover (74134a1).
- **Verified:** unit tests (236, among them two copies of the store sharing one storage); the WebKit host's suite (16 scenarios; new: `owner`, `relaunch`, `unloaded`, `slowunloaded`, `twins`; new host steps `restart`, `installTwin`, `installOld`, `show`, and a slow test page). Not yet in his Safari: TESTING.md.

## A new tab from ⌘T shows once it has loaded (2026-10-28)

- **Asked (Tirth):** ⌘T, a search or an address, Enter showed a black screen until the page came. He asked to stay on the page with Margin's empty loading screen, the new tab's row in the sidebar at once, the page fetched in the background, and shown once it's ready.
- **Why it was black:** the palette's `newTab` and `search` opened the tab in front at once (`openTab`, `active: true`); Safari shows a new tab's empty dark page until the first paint.
- **Changed:**
  - `background/tabs.js` `openBehind`: the tab opens behind the page (`tabs.create`, `active: false`), its row is written at once (in the window's space), and the page is told it's switching to it (`switching` with `cover: 'now'`). Once the tab has loaded (`status: 'complete'` at an address of its own, polled; at most 5 s, then as far as it got) it comes to the front as any switch does (woken, its sidebar prepared), without raising the window. If the user goes elsewhere meanwhile (another tab, their own row: `focusGen`), or closes it, it stays where it is and the page stops waiting without a notice.
  - `handlers.js`: `newTab` with an address, and `search` without a tab, go through it when a page asks. A search opens at its results address directly (so its row isn't "about:blank" first), recognised as Margin's own by that address (`search-engine.js` `ownSearchUrls`); Chrome's search API still starts from a blank tab.
  - Pages: the palette covers the page at once on Enter (`NEW_TAB` placeholder, `content/palette.js` `reportingSwitches`), before the tab exists (`tabs.create` took up to about 1.2 s in the WebKit host); the background's `switching` names the real tab, which the page accepts by its window before its row is in the state. `newTab` and `search` wait up to 12 s for their answer (core.js, newtab.js).
- **WebKit host:** extension-created tabs (`tabs.create`, `tabs.update` with an address, `tabs.remove`); scenarios `behind`, `behindleft`, `behindkeys`. Found while testing: in the host Margin's background stops about 5 s after it starts, with the last commit's build too, and nothing starts it again (messages then get an empty answer at once; tab events and `loadBackgroundContent` don't help). Safari wakes it for a message. Scenarios keep what needs the background within about 4 s of installing; noted in the host's README.
- **After review** (three reviewers, each finding checked by a second one; 16 of 20 held up):
  - Keys during the wait went into the page left behind (the palette gave its focus back): the palette doesn't any more, and while the switch's cover is up keys without ⌘, ⌃ or ⌥ don't reach the page; Escape calls the wait off, and so does opening the palette again (`content/core.js` `cancelSwitch`, the page's own tab asked for).
  - Only a switch in the same window calls it off (`movedOn`, per window), and so does the page the user is on starting to load something else. A switch to the new tab itself (its row clicked, a key landing on it) doesn't end the page's wait early.
  - The page's deadline bounds the whole request (`newTab`/`search` carry one, as switches do), so a slow start after Safari opens can't show "Margin isn't responding". A tab whose address Safari doesn't give (no site access) counts as loaded once seen loading, then complete. Margin's own search is known by its tab id as soon as it exists, Chrome's search row is titled with the words, and the tab opens in the page's own window.
  - Not changed: showing the page at its first paint instead of once loaded (asked for: once it's ready; for apps like YouTube the first paint is often still empty).
- **Not changed:** pins without an open tab and links still open their tab in front.
- **Verified:** unit tests (252; new: `openBehind`, the palette's placeholder, own searches by address); the WebKit suite (19 scenarios). Not yet in his Safari: TESTING.md.

## A tab shown for the first time arrives under the same cover (2026-10-29)

- **Asked (Tirth):** after the new tab had loaded behind the page (⌘T), there was still a little flicker as it came to the front (his screen recording).
- **Found:** frame by frame, 4 of 5 switches showed the whole window, sidebar included, in Safari's empty dark (#232323, not the page's own background) for 70–95 ms between the light switch cover and the page. Safari's log: as the new tab's view entered the window, `hideContentUntilPendingUpdate`, then 71 ms to its first display. WebKit's source (`WebPageProxy::dispatchActivityStateChange`) waits for the page's paint before showing a view only if that view has been in a window before; a tab loaded behind never has, so its content stays hidden until its first paint. No extension API changes that, and Safari's own ⌘-clicked tabs do the same. The gap is the page's first paint: in the WebKit host a page heavy to paint took 41–138 ms, a plain one 11 ms.
- **Changed:** a tab the background knows was never in front (opened behind, not activated since: `background/tabs.js` `neverShown`), switched to from a page that's covered (the palette's new tab, a click in the sidebar), arrives covered (`content/core.js` `arriveCovered`): prepared while hidden with the switch's cover up, spinner turning, and the page out of sight under it (`opacity: 0` on the body, or the fitted layout's card), so its first paint is the sidebar and the cover; once shown it brings the page back, lets it paint (two frames and 40 ms), and lifts the cover with its fade. At most 3 s; undone if the switch doesn't happen. In the host the heavy page's gap went to 12–18 ms, about one frame, between two frames that look the same.
- **After review** (two reviewers, each finding checked; both held up): `visibility: hidden` dropped the page's focus (a field it focused, or autofocus, lost as it was shown): `opacity` keeps it, and the first paint stays as cheap. Whether a tab was ever in front is decided by the background, per tab, not per document (a page that reloaded behind, or the reload after an update, had been in a window and doesn't need it).
- **Not changed:** about one frame of Safari's empty view remains (WebKit waits for no first paint); a tab the background didn't see open (from before it started) is taken as shown before.
- **Checked in Safari's own code** (Safari.framework from the installed system's shared cache, with REA and Hopper; its message sends resolved through the cache's selector stubs): WebKit's way to paint a view before it enters the window, `_prepareForMoveToWindow:completionHandler:`, has one caller in Safari, `-[BrowserWindowController openPreviewBrowserViewControllerInTab:andSelect:]`. Ordinary tab selection doesn't use it. An extension's `tabs.update` reaches `-[BrowserViewController activateForWebExtensionContext:completionHandler:]` → `select` → `-[BrowserWindowController selectTabViewItem:]`, the same as a click in Safari's tab bar. So no path open to an extension prepares a tab that way, and Safari's own tab bar shows the same gap for a tab loaded behind; the covered arrival is as far as Margin can take it.
- **Verified:** unit tests (253); WebKit host scenarios `behind`, `behindfitted`, `behindfocus` check the arrival (the page out of sight under the cover as it's shown, then back, focus kept), and the full suite. Not yet in his Safari: TESTING.md.

## The tabs of the space in front are kept ready to show (2026-10-29)

- **Asked (Tirth):** keep the tabs active enough that switching to them doesn't flicker; held for 30 minutes by default.
- **Found** (Safari's log, 3 hours; WebKit's source): Safari suspends a hidden tab's process a median 8 s after it's left (10% within a second), and WebKit then drops the tab's drawn layers (`WebProcessProxy::sendPrepareToSuspend` → `hideContentUntilPendingUpdate`). Of 384 tabs shown, 289 had their layers and showed with no flash (WebKit keeps the page before until they've painted); 91 had been suspended (shown dark a median 109 ms, most longer than two frames), all but 5 with no wake by Margin just before: switches Margin didn't make (Safari's own tab bar or shortcuts, most likely; the log doesn't say who switched), spread over many tabs (only 9 back to the tab just left), not closes. A process that keeps running keeps its layers; there's no lighter state between running and suspended. The only hold an extension has is a script left running in the page (what a wake does), which Safari counts as foreground work.
- **Changed:** `background/keep-ready.js`: while Safari has been used within the last `keepTabsReadyMinutes` (default 30, 0 off; Settings), the tabs of the space each window shows, not the one in front, most recently used first, at most 24, are held: the wake's self-ending script (`tabs.js` `keepAwake`, via `holdTab`), 75 s at a time, renewed when less than 20 s is left. Used means a tab switch, a window focused, or a click, key or scroll in a page (at most once a minute: `used`). The tab just left is held at once (Safari can suspend it within a second); the set follows a switch, a change of space or of the setting within 1.5 s; an alarm every minute keeps it going if Safari stops the background. Past the time, nothing more is held and the last holds end on their own. A tab Safari unloaded isn't loaded by a hold; a tab never shown still gets its one frame (above). Safari only (Chrome keeps hidden tabs' last frames).
- **Cost** (WebKit host, same engine): a busy page (a timer every 50 ms doing work, an animation, a frame loop) used 0.1 CPU-seconds in 30 s held hidden (0.3% of a core), about none left alone; WebKit already throttles hidden pages' timers and runs no frames for them. Up to 24 such pages: a few percent of a core at worst; ordinary pages much less. Safari's own numbers may differ.
- **After review** (two reviewers, each finding checked; all 11 held up): a hold ends when its tab is shown, but was still counted, so the tab just left wasn't held again for up to a minute (the back-and-forth case): a shown tab's hold is forgotten, and the tab left is held afresh. Holds placed between ticks could lapse for up to 40 s: the next tick is now set by the hold that ends first. A copy that lost the state kept holding by its own ids and its alarm kept starting it: only the owner holds, after its first sync, and a stale copy clears the alarm. A tab with no page is tried again after a minute, not every tick; ticks follow only the setting, the space shown or the tabs in it (not every title); pages say they're used only in Safari with the setting on; and a background the alarm starts again doesn't redo the Mac app report and the archive check more than every 10 minutes.
- **Not verified:** the host suspends a hidden tab later than Safari does (none in 30 s, held or not), so whether a held tab stays unsuspended was checked by unit tests and WebKit's source, not observed. In his Safari: TESTING.md.
- **Verified:** unit tests (261; new: which tabs, renewal, the time limit, off, the tab just left and left again, a hold between ticks, a tab with no page), the WebKit suite.
- **Since** (2026-11-02): at most six tabs, a tab with no page set aside until its page changes, and no hold at a background's start: "Keep-ready costs less" below.

## A tab shown for the first time is introduced first (2026-10-29)

- **Asked (Tirth):** do something about the one dark frame left as a tab that was never in front comes up (the ⌘T new tab, a ⌘-clicked link); anything goes, reverse engineering included.
- **Found** (WebKit's source, `WebPageProxy::dispatchActivityStateChange`): as any view enters the window, WebKit hides its content until the page's next paint (`hideContentUntilPendingUpdate`). A view that has been in a window before is also waited for (up to 250 ms, the window keeping its previous frame meanwhile); one that hasn't is shown at once, empty: that's the frame. Safari selects a tab as `tabs.update` asks, synchronously (its code, above), and Core Animation draws the window only once a run loop turn is over. In the WebKit host: a tab's view put in the window and taken out again in the same turn never reached the screen, and was waited for when it was shown later; the page in front saw no visibilitychange at all (WebKit tells a page about its visibility at the end of the turn, unchanged by then); two `tabs.update` sent together from Margin's background landed in the same turn.
- **Changed:** `background/tabs.js` `introduceTab`: before Margin first shows a tab it saw open behind, once its page has loaded and is awake, the background sends `tabs.update` for that tab and for the tab in front together, then (at least 60 ms later, the tab held awake again and its sidebar prepared meanwhile) shows it as before, covered arrival included. Only while Safari has the tab in front that Margin's state has (a switch on its way isn't undone). The two activations are left out of the tab events (`isIntroduction`, in order as they come, before anything else), until the real switch is sent, so the state, the window's space, the tabs kept ready and shortcut chaining see only the real switch. A page's own check for having been shown (`content/core.js` `everShown`) ignores an instant's visibility (under 200 ms), so the tab still arrives covered. The switch log says whether a switch introduced its tab. Safari only.
- **Off for the Safari session** (`storage.session`) if Safari leaves the tab in front instead (its activation is then told after all, and the tab is shown as it is), or the page in front is hidden while the two updates are in flight and back within 50 ms (`shared/switching.js` `onBlink` → `pageBlinked`, with the times: the two updates took two turns in this Safari). Not when another switch or the user's pick explains it.
- **Measured** (WebKit host, Margin's ⌘T path, a page heavy to paint): without it, 16–21 ms of WebKit's empty view each time (8 of 8 runs); with it, none in about two runs of three, at most 2 ms in the others (the commit that ends WebKit's wait can come just before the one that attaches the content: a race inside WebKit, out of an extension's reach; keeping the page busy as it's shown made it more frequent).
- **After review** (two reviewers, each finding checked; 5 held up): Safari leaving the tab in front, or the user bringing it there, had it prepared covered while on screen, blank under the spinner for 3 s: a page on screen no longer arrives covered, and the switch ends as the tab is in front. A user's switch on its way during the introduction could be undone, and its event taken for Margin's: the page in front must be the one Margin's state has, and its mark as Margin's activation is taken back once its event is seen. The success check raced a user's switch and could leave the state on the wrong tab: success is read from the events themselves, then from what's in front, and only the activation of a tab Safari left in front is told after all. The unit test for "sent together" passed with the two awaited one after the other: it checks they're in flight together now. A second review (each finding checked; 6 held up): the introduced tab was marked as Margin's own activation for 1.5 s, so a new tab's switch called off from another window left the page that asked covered, keys held, for up to 8 s: only the real switch marks it now. A real switch away and straight back within 100 ms counted as a blink and turned introductions off: only a hide while the updates are in flight counts. A second switch to the same tab could end the first one's introduction and have Safari blamed: each switch ends only its own, and a switch asked for meanwhile is never Safari's failure. Several tests passed with the checks they're named for taken out; each check now has a test that fails without it. A third review (each finding checked; 4 held up, all in the fallback paths): a tab Safari left in front while another switch started wasn't told to Margin's state, nor a user's click on it left out after the updates were answered: both are told now, as Margin's own activation. A busy page dates its visibility events late (WebKit makes them as it runs them): a blink counts from the updates going out to the real switch going out, not within 20 ms of the answers.
- **Not changed:** a tab that Safari's own tab bar or shortcuts show first isn't introduced (Margin hears of it only after); a tab still loading, or one Safari unloaded, isn't either (it arrives covered, as before).
- **Risks** (to check in Safari: TESTING.md): Safari's tab bar, if shown, selects the new tab for that instant (no frame drawn in between); the new tab may be allowed to start media for that instant, a moment before it's shown anyway (and it plays on behind if the switch is then called off).
- **Verified:** unit tests (275; new: the introduction and its order, sent together, events late or never, the user's switch in between, a switch called off or out of time, another window's switch, Safari not bringing the page back, not its doing, blinks; each check fails its test when taken out); WebKit host: `introduce` (the ⌘T path: its content as it's shown, 1–3 ms in 8 runs; the page in front not hidden by it; Margin's state never had the new tab in front before the real switch; fails without the event filter) and `arrival` (a page on screen isn't covered, one visible for an instant still is; each fails without its check); the full suite (23 scenarios, four runs: one failure of `owner` in one run, a known flake of the host's short-lived background, 5 of 5 on rerun with and without introductions). Not yet in Tirth's Safari: TESTING.md.

## A tab Safari hasn't loaded is loaded behind the page (2026-10-29)

- **Asked (Tirth, with a recording):** after quitting Safari, reopening it and installing Margin, opening tabs still flickers badly; find a solution that works every time, reverse engineering included.
- **Found** (the recording frame by frame, and Safari's log at the same moments, read with `/usr/bin/log show`): four dark gaps of 150–280 ms, the whole window dark, sidebar and all (Margin's sidebar is drawn by the page), each at the instant Safari's log has `restoreFromSessionState` for the tab being shown. Safari brings its tabs back unloaded after a relaunch (only the tab in front of each window loads) and loads one only as it's selected, so until its document arrives there's nothing to draw. Earlier this was taken as out of reach (above: `tabs.reload` doesn't load such a tab). Safari's code (Safari 27.2, `Safari.framework` from the system's shared cache, read with Hopper): `-[BrowserViewController reload]` does nothing without `canReload` (no page yet); the load starts in `-[BrowserWindowController didSelectTabViewItem]` → `beginPendingNavigation` → `_restorePendingState` → `_loadPendingSessionState` → `_restoreFromSessionStateData:expectedURL:` (its history, scroll and session storage, as when the user clicks it), the last steps after its cookies and session storage are back (60–90 ms later in Safari's log); `tabs.update` reaches it through `activateForWebExtensionContext` → `select` → `selectTabViewItem:` → `_finishLoadingTab:completionHandler:`, at once unless a selection update is still under way (then later, for the tab selected by then). No explicit screen flush on that path. `isLoadingComplete` says complete for a tab with nothing loading, so Safari reports an unloaded tab as `complete` until its load starts. And Safari puts a restored tab's view in the window before the tab has a web process: WebKit then skips it ("Not dispatching because there is no running process", for every restored tab in the log), so that first moment doesn't count as having been in a window.
- **Changed** (`background/tabs.js`, the section "a tab not ready to show"): a switch Margin makes to a tab Safari unloaded (its wake fails at once: no page) first sends `tabs.update` for that tab and for the tab in front together (`forwardAndBack`, shared with `introduceTab`): Safari starts loading it, and the page in front is back in the same run loop turn. A tab never shown that's still loading (one restored behind and clicked again, a ⌘-clicked link) is waited for the same way. Meanwhile the page in front of that window is covered by the switch's cover with its spinner (`coverFront`: told `switching`, then `switch-done` or `switch-failed`, so switches that don't cover the page themselves, a space, a closed tab, another window's tab, are covered too). Margin waits for the load to start (Safari says `loading`, or a script runs there, or doesn't answer in time, a page too busy; at most 600 ms), then for it to load (at most 2.5 s, never into the last 1.5 s of the page's wait, and not at all if less than 600 ms is left), then introduces it (even still loading) and shows it, prepared (its tab, its space, carried open, scrolled) and arriving covered, as a tab from ⌘T does. Only behind a page that says it's covered (it answers `switching`; a PDF, Safari's own pages, a site Margin can't reach or an earlier copy of Margin don't, and nothing would show for the wait: such a switch goes ahead as before). A tab in another window has its window brought forward first, so the covered page is the one the user sees. The switch log says how it went (`restored`, `loadWait`). Safari only (`platform.restoreTabs`).
- **Covered before its introduction** (second review, from Safari's log and WebKit's source): a tab that loaded behind has never painted, so the paint its introduction asks for is its first, 70–300 ms for the heavy pages in Tirth's log (8 tabs restored at 10:28); the real show went out about 60 ms after the introduction, before that paint had landed, and WebKit then doesn't hold the window for the show's paint (`hasVisibleContent()` is false while the introduction's update is pending): Safari's empty view again, shorter. So a tab that arrives covered is put out of sight under its cover first (`coverArrival`: `content/core.js` `coverArrival`, the same arrival cover, its layout done then), and only then introduced: that instant's paint, and the show's, are the cover's, which land within milliseconds. The cover ignores an instant on screen (it waits for a show that lasts). For a ⌘T tab and a ⌘-clicked link shown from the sidebar too. A reviewer's WebKit test with a page of 550 blurred layers: shown 60 ms after an introduction without it, 109–145 ms of empty view in 6 of 6 runs; covered first, WebKit held the window for its paint (9–13 ms) and showed it at once in 3 of 3.
- **When it can't** (Safari puts the load off): the switch goes ahead as before after the 600 ms, the page told which tab it is (`showing`); after two such tabs in a row (counted in `storage.session`: the background can stop between them), no more tries for the Safari session. If Safari leaves the tab in front, that's the switch (it loads there, noted, as before), and introductions and restores stop for the session, as for an introduction. A blink of the page in front (the two updates took two turns) turns them off and ends the wait under way at once.
- **Another switch meanwhile:** a click elsewhere stands; the tab stays behind, loading, and is introduced when shown later (it's taken as never shown from the moment its updates go out, so even when the switch is called off before its load is seen). A switch superseded once Safari has answered its updates no longer holds the window's turn (`windowBusy`), so a second unloaded tab clicked while the first loads is restored too; a switch asked for before Safari has answered (a double-click) waits for that answer (at most 400 ms), then for the load. A switch called off while it was still asking whether its tab has a page doesn't cover the page in front. Each covered switch says which it is (`switchId`), and a page settles only its own (`shared/switching.js`): an older switch to the same tab, called off, doesn't lift a newer one's cover. A next/previous key pressed meanwhile gives the waiting switch up (`hurry`, `background/navigation.js`): the next press goes on from that tab at once. A held key passes over tabs Safari unloaded without showing them (the highlight moves on); the tab it stops on is restored. A key press in another window gives it up too, as any switch there does (one switch at a time, the latest wins).
- **Spaces:** a space whose tab loads behind, called off from its cover (Escape), goes back to the space of the tab still in front; its tab closed meanwhile, the next one comes up, and the page isn't told the switch failed.
- **Scripts that ran:** WebKit answers a script in a tab with a view but no page yet (Safari makes a restored tab's view as it's selected, before its load) with an error in place of a result, rather than failing (`WebExtensionDynamicScriptsCocoa`: "Failed to execute script."); a page not committed yet is the empty one it starts with. Margin's wake, its check for a load started, its hold and its copy repair now look at the result (`pageThere`, `ranThere`), not only at the call settling: such a tab no longer reads as awake or started.
- **A palette tab still loading at its limit** is shown then, not waited for again (`loadWaited`: its wait was the load), and the preparation after a wait gets only what's left of the page's wait (no more 'Too late' for a busy page).
- **Not introduced only once** (first review, checked against Safari's log and WebKit's source): the restore's own moment in the window doesn't count (the view had no process yet), so a restored tab is introduced again once loaded. In the WebKit host with Safari's order (view in the window first, load after): 13–24 ms of WebKit's empty view as it was shown without that; 1–3 ms with it.
- **Measured** (WebKit host): a restored tab's content is there 1–2 ms after it's shown (`unloaded`, `unloadedtwo`); a page heavy to paint, 1–2 ms in 9 runs of 11, 8–9 ms in the other 2 (under a screen refresh: the race inside WebKit described above, out of an extension's reach); the page in front isn't hidden by either step; a slow page (1.5 s) is shown once loaded with the page in front covered meanwhile; a space chip's switch to an unloaded tab covers the page in front; Safari putting the load off falls back as before (its view made, scripts there answering with an error, as in Safari).
- **Host changes** (`tools/webkit-host`, README.md): `tabs.reload` on an unloaded tab is ignored, as Safari does; an unloaded tab is shown Safari's way (view in the window before its process, load 30 ms later); selecting a tab takes 2 ms (Safari's selection turn: 2–74 ms in its log; at the host's microseconds the two updates sometimes landed in different turns, in 2 of about 12 runs); a `deferLoads` step for Safari putting the load off, the tab's view made as it's selected all the same. New scenarios `unloadedtwo`, `unloadedheavy`, `unloadedspace`, `deferred`.
- **Not changed:** a tab Safari's own tab bar or shortcuts show first (Margin hears of it after); a page Margin can't run in.
- **Seen, not explained yet:** in the same recording, one loaded tab hidden for five minutes came up with about 60 ms of empty view, its process awake (Margin's wake ran 244 ms before; Safari's log has no suspension near it). Margin had just been reinstalled, so nothing had kept its tabs ready.
- **After review** (two rounds of reviewers, each finding checked by another; 13 held up in the first, 23 in the second, most small): the first is in the entries above (a restored tab introduced again once loaded; a key press or a second unloaded tab no longer waits behind a restore; a deadline too short for one skips it; misses counted across background restarts; switches with no cover of their own cover the page in front).
